**How to review**: tick the papers to include. To move a paper, edit its section number such as `1.2` (`+1.4` also lists it in the dataset subsection; delete that to skip it). When done, add the `apply` label to this PR. Apply writes the ticked entries into the README and pushes them to this PR; check the diff under Files changed, then merge. Unticked papers are recorded as rejected and will not be proposed again. Merge the PR even if nothing is included, so the review stays on record; do not just close it.

Stern School of Business, Department of Technology, Operations and Statistics, New York University, New York, New York 10012; Robert H. Smith School of Business, University of Maryland, College Park, Maryland 20742; Business School, Renmin University of China, Beijing 100872, China; H. John Heinz III College, Heinz College of Information Systems and Public Policy, Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania 15213 (business-school author unconfirmed)
  - Classification: If the vision language model is a substantive component, it belongs in 3.2 for prediction and targeting; the main body is currently conventional CV and deep learning, so the full text needs checking. (confidence: low)

The setting is consumer research on consumers' qualitative data, but as a methods guide without a concrete consumer task it is unclear whether it fits track C.

- C001 [Consumer insights from text analysis](https://doi.org/10.1002/jcpy.1383) — This is the introduction to a JCP special issue on text analysis, a general overview of text-analysis tools in consumer research; LLMs are mentioned only in passing, with no consumer simulation, marketing content generation or algorithm-enhancement method.
- C002 [Your Reviews Replicate You: LLM-Based Agents as Customer Digital Twins for Conjoint Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22756) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (1 citation). Affiliations: Department of Data Science, Seoul National University of Science and Technology; Department of Industrial Engineering, Seoul National University of Science and Technology. Builds LLM customer digital twins from Reddit users' comment histories plus RAG, which act as virtual conjoint respondents and are compared with real users' preferences (accuracy 87.73%); a consumer digital twin study.

Proposes a structured persona representation (BDE) and an LLM-driven structure-discovery pipeline that improve the prediction accuracy of LLM digital twins on Twin-2K-500 and a 13-sub-study benchmark. Passes the gate as a consumer digital-twin method evaluated on Twin-2K-500, the dataset the criteria name for its pricing and product questions (track A); confirm that the reported outcomes include those consumer items.
- C008 [The Prospects and Challenges of ChatGPT on Marketing Research and Practices](https://papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm?abstract_id=4398033) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C009 [Multimodal Deep Learning of Word-of-Mouth Text and Demographics to Predict Customer Rating: Handling Consumer Heterogeneity in Marketing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11888) — Only 8 pages in the arXiv PDF, references and appendices included; arXiv papers need at least 20. Fuses word-of-mouth review text with consumer demographics in a multimodal deep-learning model to predict customer ratings, a clear marketing setting. The abstract only says LLMs make this possible, so it is unclear whether an LLM is used substantively or only as a text encoder.
- C010 [SOMONITOR: Combining Explainable AI & Large Language Models for Marketing Analytics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13117) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: SoMin.ai Research, Singapore; SoMin.ai Research, Kuala Lumpur; ITMO University, Saint Petersburg; ITMO University; St. Petersburg Department of the Steklov Institute of Mathematics. SOMONITOR uses a CTR prediction model plus an LLM to extract content pillars and audience profiles from competitor content and generate content briefs; an LLM-assisted framework for marketing analytics.

- C015 [From Prompt to Purchase: How AI Brand Recommendations Move Consumers on the Open Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10907) — Studies how an AI assistant's brand recommendations affect users' web behavior; the LLM is the channel under study, it is not used to simulate consumers, and it is not a component of a marketing method, so it fits no track.
- C018 [SAGraph: A Large-Scale Social Graph Dataset with Comprehensive Context for Influencer Selection in Marketing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15105) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Gaoling School of Artificial Intelligence, Renmin University of China; Moonshot AI; Mohamed bin Zayed University of Artificial Intelligence. - C112 [Probable but Not Valid: A Psychometric Audit of Decision Models as Synthetic Survey Respondents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14912) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Audits decision models and generative LLMs as synthetic respondents on a public Lithuanian organizational-psychology questionnaire against statistical baselines. Organizational-psychology survey with no consumer or marketing outcome.
- C113 [TV Advertising Effectiveness with Racial Minority Representation: Evidence from the Mortgage Market](https://papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm?abstract_id=4521178) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C114 [Behavior Optimized Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.10995) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Adobe Media and Data Science Research; IIIT-Delhi; SUNY at Buffalo; MBZUAI. Proposes an LLM that understands images and user behavior and uses it to generate behavior-optimized images that improve KPIs such as clicks; marketing creative optimization. Affiliations include Adobe and universities; business-school authors to be checked.
- C115 [CFaiRLLM: Consumer Fairness Evaluation in Large-Language Model Recommender System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05668) — A general framework for evaluating fairness in LLM recommender systems; it has no marketing context and is not a consumer simulation, so it is a general recommender-systems paper that fits no track.
- C116 [Virtual Personas for Language Models via an Anthology of Backstories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06576) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). Affiliations: University of California, Berkeley; University of California, Berkeley and Google LLC. Proposes the Anthology method, which conditions an LLM on backstories and matches real human response distributions in three ATP survey waves; a human-simulation method on public data.
- C118 [Esports Debut as a Medal Event at 2023 Asian Games: Exploring Public Perceptions with BERTopic and GPT-4 Topic Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18798) — A case analysis of public views on esports at the Asian Games; the LLM is used only for topic labeling, with no substantive contribution on marketing methods or content generation, so it fits none of the tracks.
- C119 [Measuring and Improving Persuasiveness of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02653) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliation: Adobe Media & Data Science Research. Proposes a persuasiveness evaluation benchmark and improves LLM persuasive-language generation through targeted training, with scenarios including advertising and marketing; a content generation method. The authors come from Adobe and others; business-school authors to be checked.
- C121 [Leveraging Large Language Models for Institutional Portfolio Management: Persona-Based Ensembles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19515) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C122 [Powering LLM Regulation through Data: Bridging the Gap from Compute Thresholds to Customer Experiences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03472) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C123 [Not all AI is created equal: A meta-analysis revealing drivers of AI resistance across markets, methods, and time](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijresmar.2025.02.005) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C124 [Towards Equitable AI: Detecting Bias in Using Large Language Models for Marketing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12838) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Horace Mann School; Columbia University and Arab American University (department not printed on the first page; the AAUP personal CV shows an adjunct AI instructor at Columbia and assistant professor at AAUP, teaching machine learning, with a civil-engineering background). Generates 1,700 financial marketing slogans aimed at different demographic groups and uses KS tests to detect thematic bias; a study of limitations and fairness in marketing content generation; business-school authors to be checked.
- C125 [Can Large Language Models Simulate Human Responses? A Case Study of Stated Preference Experiments in the Context of Heating-related Choices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10652) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (3 citations); affiliation: Centre for Transport Engineering and Modelling, Department of Civil and Environmental Engineering, Imperial College London. Evaluates how well several LLMs simulate SP choice experiments at the individual and aggregate levels and explores combining them with traditional choice models; consumer choice simulation, but the setting is energy products rather than typical marketing.
- C126 [PersonaBOT: Bringing Customer Personas to Life with LLMs and RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17156) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (7 citations); affiliations: Department of Computer Science, Jönköping University; Volvo Construction Equipment; Department of Computer Science, Jönköping University; Department of Future Solutions, Volvo Construction Equipment. Generates and evaluates synthetic customer profiles, but the profiles are used for an internal decision-support chatbot and do not simulate customers' answers or choices; it is uncertain whether this belongs to the consumer simulation track.
- C127 [Semiotic Reconstruction of Destination Expectation Constructs An LLM-Driven Computational Paradigm for Social Media Tourism Analytics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16118) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). Affiliation: College of Horticulture Landscape Architecture, Northeast Agricultural University, Harbin, China. Proposes an LLM framework with unsupervised extraction plus survey-supervised fine-tuning to quantify tourist destination expectations for personalized marketing; a consumer text measurement method. The authors may come from an engineering/agricultural college and need verification.
- C128 [LLMs for Customized Marketing Content Generation and Evaluation at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17863) — Only 16 pages in the arXiv PDF, references and appendices included; arXiv papers need at least 20. Proposes MarketingFM, a retrieval-augmented LLM that generates keyword-level off-site e-commerce ad copy, validated with online A/B tests showing higher click-through, plus an automatic LLM-judge evaluation system. Advertising content generation with a market outcome is track C.
- C129 [ChatGPT is not A Man but Das Man: Representativeness and Structural Consistency of Silicon Samples Generated by Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02919) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Generates GPT-4 and Llama 3.1 silicon samples on ANES 2020 items and finds structural inconsistency across demographic levels and homogenized minority opinions. Political survey simulation, so it fails the gate.
- C130 [ECom-Bench: Can LLM Agent Resolve Real-World E-commerce Customer Support Issues?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05639) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C131 [Prompt Perturbations Reveal Human-Like Biases in Large Language Model Survey Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07188) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (15 citations); affiliations: University of Mannheim; University of Mannheim; GESIS - Leibniz Institute for the Social Sciences; Complexity Science Hub, Vienna. Applies 10 types of prompt perturbation to 9 LLMs on WVS items to examine the robustness and human-like response biases of simulated respondents; the abstract does not state whether the results are compared against real human WVS answer distributions, so it is uncertain.
- C132 [TinyTroupe: An LLM-powered Multiagent Persona Simulation Toolkit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.09788) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (10 citations); affiliations: Microsoft Corporation; Dipeak Technology, Beijing (work done while at Microsoft). TinyTroupe is a general LLM multi-agent persona-simulation toolkit, with brainstorming and market-research sessions as examples and a preliminary comparison against humans; it is unclear whether it targets consumer simulation specifically, so it is uncertain.
- C133 [Agentic Personalized Fashion Recommendation in the Age of Generative AI: Challenges, Opportunities, and Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.02342) — An opinion and research-agenda paper on fashion recommender systems that proposes an agentic hybrid-modality refinement pipeline, but the abstract has no empirical work at the marketing-method level; a recommender-system engineering paper.
- C134 [Reference Points in LLM Sentiment Analysis: The Role of Structured Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11454) — Only 9 pages in the arXiv PDF, references and appendices included; arXiv papers need at least 20. Tests whether adding reference-point information, motivated by prospect theory and expectation-disconfirmation theory, and its prompt format (natural language versus JSON) improve LLM sentiment analysis of Yelp reviews. Passes the gate as a customer-review measurement method (track C, category 3).
- C136 [RecMind: LLM-Enhanced Graph Neural Networks for Personalized Consumer Recommendations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06286) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C137 [The threat of analytic flexibility in using large language models to simulate human data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13397) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (31 citations); affiliations: University of Bern, Switzerland; University of Oxford, United Kingdom (visiting). Systematically examines how analytic choices in silicon-sample modeling (model, sampling, prompting) affect the fidelity of results and re-examines Argyle et al.'s Study 3; the abstract does not name the public human datasets used, so it is uncertain.
- C138 [LLMs on Drugs: Language Models Are Few-Shot Consumers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18546) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C139 [Recontextualizing Famous Quotes for Brand Slogan Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06049) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). Affiliations: Brandeis University (Liu lab, computer science; department not printed); Adobe; Michtom School of Computer Science, Brandeis University. Proposes a modular LLM framework that generates brand slogans by recontextualizing famous quotes, with automatic and human evaluation; an ad creative generation method.
- C140 [Culturally Grounded Personas in Large Language Models: Characterization and Alignment with Socio-Psychological Value Frameworks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22396) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (3 citations). Affiliation: DIMES (Dept. of Computer Engineering, Modeling, Electronics and Systems Engineering), University of Calabria. Builds cultural personas from WVS variables and compares them with the WVS population response distributions and value frameworks, testing how well LLM personas align with cultural value systems; an evaluation of human simulation on public data.
- C142 [Auditing Preferences for Brands and Cultures in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18300) — ChoiceEval audits the LLM's own preferences for brands and regions; the LLM is the object of study and neither simulates consumers nor is compared with real human data, a case of blanket exclusion.
- C144 [A Modular LLM Framework for Explainable Price Outlier Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20636) — An LLM agent pipeline for price anomaly detection in e-commerce catalogs; operational quality-control engineering, not marketing pricing research, and unrelated to consumer simulation.
- C145 [Helping Customers in Distress: An LLM-powered Agent that Converses, Probes, and Routes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16268) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C146 [Can Persona-Prompted LLMs Emulate Subgroup Values? An Empirical Analysis of Generalisability and Fairness in Cultural Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12851) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Singapore University of Technology and Design (SUTD); A*STAR, Singapore; A*STAR, Singapore; CNRS@CREATE, Singapore; Microsoft Research Asia (MSRA); Singapore University of Technology and Design (SUTD). Uses WVS Singapore data to test whether persona-prompted LLMs can simulate the values of demographic subgroups, improves accuracy through fine-tuning, and reveals fairness gaps between subgroups; an evaluation of human simulation on public data.
- C148 [PrefBench: Evaluating Zero-Shot LLM Agents in Hidden-Preference Personalized Pricing Negotiations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22855) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliation: Department of Computing Science, University of Aberdeen. PrefBench evaluates LLM seller agents in personalized pricing negotiation under hidden buyer preferences; the buyers are driven by a rule-based simulator, so whether it counts as a contribution at the marketing-method level is uncertain, hence uncertain.
- C149 [In-Context Learning for the Imputation of Public Opinion Data with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09351) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (2 citations). Affiliations: LMU Munich; Munich Center for Machine Learning; University of Mannheim; LMU Munich; University of Mannheim; Institute for Employment Research (IAB); LMU Munich; Munich Center for Machine Learning; University of Maryland, College Park. Proposes using LLM in-context learning to impute missing survey responses and compares it with MICE PMM on 150 items across 15 ATP waves; a method for LLM prediction of individual responses on public survey data.
- C150 [Simulated Customers Never Walk Away: Decision Fidelity of LLM User Simulators Measured Against Real Purchase Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20708) — Only 19 pages in the arXiv PDF, references and appendices included; arXiv papers need at least 20. Tests LLM customer simulators against real sales conversations with verified payment outcomes and finds they overstate the purchase propensity of non-buyers. Simulating customers' purchase decisions against real outcomes is a track A validity study.
- C151 [Tastes without distinction: silicon samples and the synthetic construction of tastes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30085) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Generates over 550,000 silicon respondents to Survey of Public Participation in the Arts items and compares their cultural tastes and taste structure with real respondents. A sociology-of-taste study on an arts-participation survey with no product, brand, pricing or other marketing outcome.
- C152 [Distribution-First Population Simulation: Collapse, Calibration, and Recall in Non-WEIRD LLM Persona Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18310) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Measures distribution collapse of LLM persona agents on WVS Turkey microdata and tests a verbalized-sampling correction. - C153 [What Users Think of Generative AI: A Cross-Platform NLP Analysis of Trust and Friction in App Store Reviews](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19151) — Analyzes app-store reviews of generative AI applications; the LLM is only the product being studied, and the analysis uses BERTopic and a RoBERTa encoder without substantive LLM use, a case of blanket exclusion.
- C155 [Efficient Clustering with Quality Guardrails for LLM-based Recommender Systems at Industry Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19704) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C156 [Large language models simulate intersectional synthetic identities with a budget of one to two dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23005) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (1 citation). Affiliations: Massachusetts Institute of Technology (MIT); the author's homepage shows an MIT GOV/LAB postdoc; École Polytechnique. Uses 8 LLMs on 15 ATP waves to simulate response distributions for all intersectional subgroups and finds the simulations fail to reproduce the real additive and intersectional structure; a simulation validity study on public data.
- C157 [Total Simulated Survey Error: Designing and Diagnosing Survey Responses from Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10280) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: University of Mannheim; GESIS - Leibniz Institute for the Social Sciences; University of Graz; University of Duisburg-Essen…. Proposes the TS2E framework, systematically laying out conceptual errors and systematic biases at each stage of LLM-generated survey responses, with case studies; the abstract does not name the public human data used, so uncertain.
- C158 [Evaluating Brand Retrieval and Ranking in Large Language Model Recommendations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16304) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Proposes a sampling framework to measure how likely six LLMs are to recommend and rank brands across five product categories. - C159 [$SmartProbe$: A Virtual Moderator for Market Research Surveys](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.08271) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Nexxt Intelligence; University of Waterloo. SmartProbe uses an LLM to generate follow-up questions in market-research questionnaires, acting as a virtual moderator, and evaluates follow-up quality; a market-research measurement method.
- C160 [Persona-DB: Efficient Large Language Model Personalization for Response Prediction with Collaborative Data Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11060) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (65 citations). Affiliations: University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign; University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign / Amazon; Amazon. Persona-DB uses retrieval augmentation and collaborative refinement so that an LLM personalizes predictions of users' opinions and preferences; the abstract does not name the human response data used and it is not a marketing setting, so it is uncertain.
- C163 [The Influencer Next Door: How Misinformation Creators Use GenAI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13554) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C164 ["Global is Good, Local is Bad?": Understanding Brand Bias in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13997) — Examines the LLM's own brand biases; the LLM is the object of study and neither simulates consumers nor is compared with real human data, a case of blanket exclusion.
- C165 [USDC: A Dataset of $\underline{U}$ser $\underline{S}$tance and $\underline{D}$ogmatism in Long $\underline{C}$onversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16833) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C167 [Dynamic Sentiment Analysis with Local Large Language Models using Majority Voting: A Study on Factors Affecting Restaurant Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13069) — Only 14 pages in the arXiv PDF, references and appendices included; arXiv papers need at least 20. Proposes majority voting over repeated sentiment labels from local LLMs and shows on online restaurant reviews that a mid-sized model with several votes is more robust than a large model with one pass, then studies factors behind restaurant evaluations. Passes the gate as a customer-review analysis method (track C, category 3).
- C168 [CXSimulator: A User Behavior Simulation using LLM Embeddings for Web-Marketing Campaign Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21553) — Represents user behavior events with LLM embeddings and trains a transition prediction model to simulate campaign effects; the LLM is only a text encoder, not an LLM simulating consumers, so it does not meet the requirement of substantive LLM use.
- C169 [Movie Gen: SWOT Analysis of Meta's Generative AI Foundation Model for Transforming Media Generation, Advertising, and Entertainment Industries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03837) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C170 [CTR-Driven Advertising Image Generation with Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06823) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Huazhong University of Science and Technology (interning at JD.COM); JD.COM; Huazhong University of Science and Technology; Sun Yat-sen University. - C171 [Comments on “ AI and the advent of the cyborg behavioral scientist”](https://doi.org/10.1002/jcpy.1453) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.

Affiliations: Northeastern University; Amazon.com, Inc. Uses real online shopping session data to quantitatively evaluate the accuracy with which LLM agents simulate customers' step-by-step shopping behavior; finds prompting alone reaches only 11.86% and fine-tuning improves it; a validity study of consumer simulation.
- C173 [PAARS: Persona Aligned Agentic Retail Shoppers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.24228) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Amazon. Mines personas from historical shopping data to build shopping agents and proposes a population-level distribution-alignment evaluation suite compared with real human behavior; consumer simulation.
- C174 [Expanding the Generative AI Design Space through Structured Prompting and Multimodal Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14320) — A GenAI interaction interface for ad creation by small-business owners and an HCI formative study; the contribution is in interaction design rather than marketing methods or consumer simulation, so it fits no track.

Uses the public ANES and Zuobiao survey data to compare the ability of several Chinese and US LLMs to simulate public opinion and analyzes within-group over-homogenization; an evaluation of human simulation on public data.

Uses a PWYW pricing experiment with 522 real people to evaluate an LLM's ability to predict individual payment decisions, finding poor individual-level but acceptable group-level performance; a validity study of consumer simulation.

Uses persona-conditioned SFT and RLAIF to generate personalized product review summaries that support purchase decisions; personalized generation and customer text.
- C185 [Reducing False Positives in Static Bug Detection with LLMs: An Empirical Study in Industry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18844) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.

- C189 [Decoding the Hook: A Multimodal LLM Framework for Analyzing the Hooking Period of Video Ads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22299) — Only 11 pages in the arXiv PDF, references and appendices included; arXiv papers need at least 20. Uses a multimodal LLM to extract features from the first three seconds (the hooking period) of video ads and links them to conversion performance on a social-media ad platform. Ad creative measurement with conversion outcomes is track C.
- C190 [Multi-Agent Large Language Model Based Emotional Detoxification Through Personalized Intensity Control for Consumer Protection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23123) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.

- C193 [Detecting RAG Advertisements Across Advertising Styles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04925) — Studies the robustness of detectors for generative native ads in RAG responses, treating the LLM as an advertising channel; a classification/detection task that is neither a marketing method nor consumer simulation.
- C194 [Expert Personas Improve LLM Alignment but Damage Accuracy: Bootstrapping Intent-Based Persona Routing with PRISM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18507) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.

- C196 [Prominence-Stratified Failure Modes in Retrieval-Augmented Commercial Recommendation: A 37,000-Run Audit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27439) — An audit of brand exposure and win rates in RAG commercial recommendation; it treats the LLM as the recommendation channel under study, does not simulate consumers, and is not a component of a marketing method.
- C197 [Testing the Black Box: Structural Barriers to Independent Evaluation of Consumer-Facing Health LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08483) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.

- C199 [LLM Consumer Behavior Theory: Foundations of a Novel Research Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18005) — Only 7 pages in the arXiv PDF, references and appendices included; arXiv papers need at least 20. Conceptual paper proposing an 'LLM consumer behavior theory' that formalizes LLM agents making consumption decisions for users and integrates work on LLM decision-making, human-behavior simulation and preference elicitation, with no empirical part. The consumer-decision setting is clear, but whether it fits track A is unclear, since it treats LLMs as consumer agents rather than as simulations of real consumers.
- C200 [Can LLMs Reason About Brand Ownership? An Empirical Study of Domain Attribution Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20868) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C201 [How Large Language Models Source Brand Reputation Across Languages and Markets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25787) — Counts the distribution of cited sources when LLMs answer about brand reputation, treating the LLM as an information channel under study; there are no simulated consumers and it is not a component of a marketing method.
- C202 [CoPersona: Collaborative Persona Graphs for Robust LLM Personalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01485) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C204 [Where Does the Noise Come From? A Variance-Components Decomposition of Non-Determinism in LLM Brand Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13304) — A decomposition of variance in LLM brand answers, aimed at measuring the stability of LLM outputs about brands; it treats the LLM as the object under test, does not simulate consumers, and is not a component of a marketing method.
- C208 [PILA: Plug-and-Play Insertion for LLM-native Advertising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25590) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Institute for AI, Peking University; College of AI, Tsinghua University; University of Michigan (no school/department printed); State Key Laboratory of General AI, BIGAI…. Models LLM-native ad insertion as conditional response rewriting, controllably trading off naturalness against exposure; ad content generation, but also leaning toward LLM-platform monetization.
- C209 [LLM-OSDA: An Optimal-Stopping Dynamic Auction for Native Advertising in Multi-Turn LLM Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00123) — The core is a dynamic auction mechanism with optimal stopping in multi-turn LLM conversations, studying the LLM as a monetization platform; the LLM is not a substantive component of a marketing method.
- C210 [The Beginning of ChatGPT Ads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05008) — Uses sock-puppet audits to examine demographic differences in the ads that ChatGPT users see, treating the LLM interface as the ad market under study; there are no simulated consumers and it is not a marketing method.
- C211 [Computational KJ-Ho: An Analyst-Bias-Free Insight Extraction Framework from Large-Scale Qualitative Data Using Domain-Specialized LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16467) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). Affiliation: Sophia University Graduate School, Graduate Program in Applied Data Sciences. Proposes an LLM qualitative data analysis framework for consumer insights; a customer-text method, but only a conceptual paper with no empirical validation yet.
- C212 [DeepAffinity: Long-Term Aspect Preference Prediction in eCommerce using Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02468) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). Affiliations: eBay Inc.; Ben-Gurion University; DREAM group (work done while at eBay Inc.). Fine-tunes small language models to predict e-commerce users' preferences for attributes such as brand, size and color and improves recommendation quality; a preference prediction method.
- C213 [The Dice Roll Method: A Standardized Protocol for Repeated-Query Auditing of Large Language Model Brand Recommendations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04047) — Proposes a statistical protocol that audits the stability of LLM brand recommendations through repeated queries, treating the LLM as the object under test; there are no simulated consumers and it is not a component of a marketing method.
- C214 [Who Bears the Risk When Generative AI Enters Transport? A Distributional Sociotechnical Audit of Algorithmic Equity, Synthetic-Data Validity, and Public Trust](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11611) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.
- C215 [Market Signal Injection: Adversarial Context Manipulation of LLM Pricing Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18357) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). Affiliation: Graduate School of Data Science, Seoul National University; Graduate School of Data Science, Seoul National University (no separate affiliation printed; snu.ac.kr email; SNU GSDS faculty page lists him as associate professor there; earlier at Ohio State Fisher College of Business 2017-2021, not current). Proposes a market signal injection attack that tests the sensitivity of LLM pricing agents to data presentation in a simulated Bertrand market, and offers defenses. The LLM is a component of a pricing algorithm, but the work looks more like security/adversarial robustness research, so its status as a marketing method is uncertain.
- C216 [OneBid: A Unified Auto-Bidding Foundation Model for Diverse oCPX Advertising Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21550) — Initial screening (title plus abstract snippet) judged that it does not fit any of the three inclusion tracks.

The setting is marketing, but the LLM agents are only the object audited, neither simulating human consumers (no human comparison) nor serving as a marketing method, so it falls under the always-exclude rule.
- C218 [A Systematic Investigation of Bias in Large Language Models for Advertising Relevance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07544) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). Affiliation: Microsoft. Uses a counterfactual framework to systematically test LLM bias in ad-query relevance judgments with respect to advertiser identity, language and demographic wording, and studies mitigation. The topic is LLM algorithm evaluation in advertising, category 3.
- C219 [LLM-based User-Product Relevance Evaluation for Dynamic Product Advertising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14550) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Snap Inc. Proposes a user-item relevance metric using an LLM as relevance judge to evaluate a dynamic product ad retrieval system offline, correlated with online business metrics. The topic is a measurement method for ad retrieval evaluation, with the LLM as a substantive component.

The LLM is only the object being measured (brand visibility), there are no simulated consumers, and it is not a component of a marketing method.

- C227 [Comparing Traditional and LLM-based Search for Consumer Choice: A Randomized Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.03744) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Microsoft Research, New York City. A meta-evaluation of whether LLMs can replace humans in judging the serendipity of recommender systems, compared with humans on two user-study datasets (e-commerce and movies); the best correlation is only 21.5%. - C275 [PosterMate: Audience-driven Collaborative Persona Agents for Poster Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18572) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (18 citations). - C276 [Vocalize: Lead Acquisition and User Engagement through Gamified Voice Competitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20730) — Introduces Vocalize, a system that gathers leads through voice games, and reports a user study from 4 events; a product system description in which the LLM is only an auxiliary component, not marketing method research.
- C277 [MaRGen: Multi-Agent LLM Approach for Self-Directed Market Research and Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01370) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Okinawa Institute of Science and Technology; Institute of Science Tokyo; Amazon, Tokyo. MaRGen uses multi-agent LLMs to automatically query data, analyze and generate market-research reports, and proposes an LLM evaluation and iterative-improvement mechanism consistent with expert ratings. It leans toward general business-analytics automation, and its marketing-method character and the authors' background are unclear.
- C281 [Inducing State Anxiety in LLM Agents Reproduces Human-Like Biases in Consumer Decision-Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.06222) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (6 citations). Has three LLMs do a shopping task under a budget constraint; anxiety narratives significantly lower the basket health score, reproducing the consumption bias of anxious humans. A study of bias in LLM consumer behavior, not compared directly with human data.
- C284 [M-PACE: Mother Child Framework for Multimodal Compliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15241) — Proposes using parent-child multimodal LLMs for ad compliance review and releases a human-annotated benchmark; an engineering pipeline for content moderation that is neither content generation and personalization nor a marketing decision method, so it fits no track.
- C286 [Accelerate Creation of Product Claims Using Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20652) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Department of Computer Science, University of Cincinnati; Corporate Functions R&D, Procter & Gamble; Beauty Care IT, Procter & Gamble; Beauty Care R&D, Procter & Gamble. - C287 [MADS: Multi-Agent Dialogue Simulation for Diverse Persuasion Data Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05124) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: Baidu Inc., Beijing. - C288 [Galton's Law of Mediocrity: Why Large Language Models Regress to the Mean and Fail at Creativity in Advertising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25767) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). - C475 [When Can Digital Personas Reliably Approximate Human Survey Findings?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10659) — No business school authors and insufficient impact (4 citations); affiliations: Department of Mathematics and Statistics, York University; Department of Mathematics and Statistics, York University; Department of Biostatistics, University Health Network. Systematically evaluates the hypothesis that more detailed LLM personas are better, finding "persona manifold collapse": simple age-and-gender personas outperform detailed ideal-customer profiles; a study of the limits of persona simulation.
- C477 [Narrative Sharpens Gender Gaps: Surveying Film Characters with LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22091) — No business-school authors and insufficient influence (0 citations). Affiliation: Computer and Information Science, University of Pennsylvania. Turns movie characters into interviewable LLM agents that answer WVS gender-attitude questions and compares them with real surveys; the aim is to measure film and TV cultural content rather than simulate real human populations, so it is uncertain whether it counts as human simulation.

- C480 [Whose hotel does the AI recommend? An algorithm audit of reputation signals in LLM-assisted hotel selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16344) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses a randomized conjoint to audit how 12 LLM assistants weight ratings, price and ranking position when recommending hotels, without human comparison data. The LLM is only the object audited, like the brand-exposure audits on the always-exclude list (and the removed crowdfunding audit by the same design), so it is excluded despite the hotel-choice setting.
- C481 [HOBA: Hierarchical On-Policy Bidding Agents for Adaptive Online Advertising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24779) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliation: Kuaishou Technology, Beijing. A hierarchical reinforcement-learning framework for online ad bidding in which the LLM only infers high-level hyperparameters, so it reads more like ad-tech engineering; topically it barely falls in 3.2 (online learning and advertising), and it needs confirmation of whether there are business-school authors.
- C482 [ExpertIVS: Sociological Expert Driven Individual Value Simulation in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20355) — No business-school authors and insufficient impact (0 citations); affiliations: International Joint Institute of Tianjin University, Fuzhou; TJUNLP Lab, School of Computer Science and Technology, Tianjin University; National Governance Institute, Tianjin Normal University; College of Computer and Information Engineering, Tianjin Normal University. Proposes the ExpertIVS framework, which uses 14 sociological expert agents to reconstruct WVS answers into individual value profiles and evaluates value-recovery fidelity on 480 people in 12 countries; an individual-simulation method based on public survey data.
- C483 [Generative Engine Optimization at Scale: Measuring Brand Visibility Across AI Search Engines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20065) — Measures how AI search engines such as ChatGPT and Perplexity mention and cite brands, treating the LLM as the platform under study; the LLM is not used to simulate consumers and is not a component of a marketing method.

- C485 [The Language Blind Spot: How Query Language and Brand Recognition Tier Shape AI-Constructed Brand Reputation Across Twelve European Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23165) — Measures differences in how LLMs describe brand reputation across query languages, treating the LLM as a monitored product or platform; there are no simulated consumers and no comparison against human data.

Uses public Upworthy headline A/B click-through data as ground truth to test whether LLM synthetic personas predict real audience response to copy variants, and finds a no-persona baseline does better. Audience response to marketing copy; track A validity study.

- U053 [The Indirect Disclosure Effect: How Disclosing Generative AI Use Impacts Human Creative Collaboration with AI](https://doi.org/10.1287/isre.2024.0951) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Two text-to-image experiments on how disclosing GenAI use affects creators' collaboration with AI and audience evaluations. Fails the gate: creative collaboration and disclosure, no consumer or marketing outcome.
- U054 [Generative AI, Platform Stances, and Content Creator Behavior](https://doi.org/10.1287/isre.2025.2155) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Compares creator activity on two visual-art platforms that adopted or banned AI image generation. Platform stances toward GenAI and creator behavior are not a consumer or marketing setting, and GenAI is only the object studied.
- U055 [Computational Creativity in the AI Era: A Research Agenda for Information Systems](https://doi.org/10.1287/isre.2026.editorial.v37.n4) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): ISR editorial introducing a special issue on computational creativity. No marketing setting.
- U056 [Unlocking Profits in Generative AI: The Impact of AI Adaptive Learning on Freemium Strategy](https://doi.org/10.1287/isre.2025.2140) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Analytical model of how generative AI's adaptive learning changes a firm's freemium strategy and consumer surplus. Always excluded: the GenAI product is only the market studied (pricing theory for LLM platforms).
- U057 [The Impact of Generative AI on Collaborative Open-Source Software Development: Evidence from GitHub Copilot](https://doi.org/10.1287/isre.2024.1361) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses GitHub Copilot usage data to study how AI pair programming affects open-source contributions and coordination costs. Software development is not a marketing setting.
- U059 [Seeing Less, Engaging More: Rethinking Early User Experience on Generative AI Cocreation Platforms—Findings from a Field Experiment](https://doi.org/10.1287/isre.2024.1200) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Field experiment with more than 20,000 new users of a GenAI co-creation platform on how output display and sign-up framing affect registration and engagement. Always excluded: the GenAI service is only the product studied; the LLM neither simulates consumers nor serves a marketing method.
- U060 [“Generate” the Future of Work through AI: Empirical Evidence from Online Labor Markets](https://doi.org/10.1287/isre.2023.0461) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Studies how LLM-based generative AI changes demand, supply and competition across skill submarkets of an online labor platform. Labor markets are not a marketing setting.
- U061 [Interactivity and Illusions of Ability: How Using Generative AI Affects Investor Judgments](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70017) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Three experiments on how generative AI's interactivity inflates investors' perceived ability and willingness to invest. Investor judgment with no marketing setting.
- U062 [Human Capital Disclosure and Labor Market Outcomes: Evidence from Regulation S‐K](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70036) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses a generative LLM to measure DEI disclosure in job postings and tests the labor-market effects of Regulation S-K. Fails the gate: accounting and labor markets, no marketing setting.
- U063 [Generative AI in Financial Reporting](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70050) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Evaluates commercial GenAI-detection tools and estimates the share of GenAI-written text in financial reports, earnings calls and risk-factor disclosures. Corporate financial disclosure is not a marketing setting.
- U064 [How Stock Market Participants Use Generative Artificial Intelligence: Evidence from User‐Platform Interaction Data](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70051) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses 1.7 million stock-related queries on a Chinese GenAI platform to describe how investors use GenAI and how it affects liquidity and informed trading. Finance with no marketing setting.
- U065 [Human + AI in Accounting: Early Evidence from the Field](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70052) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Survey, field and experimental evidence on how GenAI affects accountants' efficiency and reporting quality. Fails the gate: accounting work, no marketing setting.
- U066 [Generative AI in Capital Markets: Information Production, Dissemination, and Processing](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70061) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Reviews six papers from the 2025 JAR conference on how GenAI reshapes information production, dissemination and processing in capital markets. Capital markets are not a marketing setting.
- U067 [AI Democratization and Trading Inequality](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70063) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses ChatGPT's launch to study how retail trades align with AI sentiment signals and what this means for information asymmetry and trading profits. Finance with no marketing setting.
- U068 [Generative AI Use by Capital Market Information Intermediaries: Evidence from Seeking Alpha](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70053) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Studies AI-generated articles on Seeking Alpha and their effects on author productivity, trading volume and price discovery. Fails the gate: capital-market information, no marketing setting.
- U069 [Caution Ahead: Numerical Reasoning and Look‐Ahead Bias in AI Models](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70058) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Tests LLMs' numerical-reasoning failures and look-ahead bias in accounting and finance prediction tasks. Accounting and finance tasks are not a marketing setting.
- U070 [Measuring Organizational Capital](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70073) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Builds a measure of organizational capital from Glassdoor employee reviews with word embeddings and ChatGPT-generated synthetic reviews. Employee data, not customers, so no marketing setting.
- U071 [Beyond Automation: AI and the Human Value of Sell‐Side Analysts](https://doi.org/10.1111/1475-679x.70083) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Studies how investment-bank AI investment affects sell-side analysts' information acquisition and forecast quality, with GenAI only in a supplementary test. Fails the gate: financial analysts, no marketing setting.
- U072 [The impact of generative AI on information processing: Evidence from the ban of ChatGPT in Italy](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jacceco.2025.101782) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses Italy's ChatGPT ban as a natural experiment to study generative AI's effect on information processing (abstract missing). Information processing in capital markets is not a marketing setting.
- U073 [Does generative AI facilitate investor Trading? Early evidence from ChatGPT outages](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jacceco.2025.101821) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses ChatGPT outages to test whether investors rely on GenAI for trading and how this affects price informativeness. Finance with no marketing setting.
- U074 [Generative AI and investor processing of financial information](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jacceco.2026.101908) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Title-only entry on how generative AI affects investors' processing of financial information. Fails the gate: investor behavior, no marketing setting.
- U076 [Understanding and Improving Consumer Reactions to Service Bots](https://doi.org/10.1093/jcr/ucad023) — Studies consumers' negative evaluations of humanoid service robots and digital chatbots and the mechanisms behind them; the focus is service automation rather than generative AI or LLMs, and it was published before LLMs became widespread, so it is not included.
- U078 [GenAI and Consumer Research: Are We the Last Generation of Human Consumer Researchers?](https://doi.org/10.1093/jcr/ucaf015) — An introductory editorial for the JCR special issue on "GenAI and consumer research" that merely calls for a critical approach to GenAI and has no standalone substantive research content, so it is not included.
- U082 [AI Companions Reduce Loneliness](https://doi.org/10.1093/jcr/ucaf040) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Review data, experiments and a longitudinal study show that AI companion chatbots reduce loneliness. Fails the gate: the AI companion is only the product studied and the outcome is psychological well-being, not a marketing outcome.
- U083 [Brief Commentary: A Framework for Detecting AI Agents in Online Research](https://doi.org/10.1093/jcr/ucag006) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Proposes a "cognitive trap" framework that uses structural limits of vision-language models to design survey items that detect AI agents such as ChatGPT Agent answering online studies. It addresses data quality in online research in general; the LLM is a threat to detect rather than a simulated consumer or a marketing method, and the venue alone does not pass the gate.
- U085 [Debt dynamics and credit risk](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfineco.2023.06.007) — Studies the effect of corporate debt dynamics on credit-risk pricing; unrelated to LLMs or generative AI, so not included.
- U086 [Global Business Networks](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfineco.2025.104007) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses GPT-3 business descriptions, OpenAI embeddings and fine-tuned language models to build global firm networks. Fails the gate: finance, no marketing setting.
- U087 [Intellectual property protection lost and competition: An examination using large language models](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfineco.2026.104306) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses an LLM to measure firms' patent exposure to Supreme Court rulings and tests how lost IP protection affects innovation, competition and valuation. Patents and competition are not a marketing setting.
- U088 [Can ChatGPT forecast stock price movements? Return predictability and large language models](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfineco.2026.104335) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Studies whether ChatGPT can forecast stock price movements. Finance with no marketing setting.
- U089 [Chronologically consistent large language models](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfineco.2026.104374) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Trains chronologically consistent language models (ChronoBERT, ChronoGPT) to remove look-ahead bias in asset pricing. - U090 [How to intelligently embrace generative AI: the first guardrails for the use of GenAI in IB research](https://doi.org/10.1057/s41267-024-00736-0) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): JIBS editorial guidelines for using GenAI in international business research (abstract missing). No marketing setting.
- U091 [Business and management research on societal wellbeing: a historic, multi-journal and large language model analysis of the UN’s Sustainable Development Goals](https://doi.org/10.1057/s41267-025-00833-8) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses an LLM to analyze business and management research on the UN Sustainable Development Goals across journals and decades. - U093 [Sponsored Content as an Epistemic Market Object: How Platformization of Brand–Creator Partnerships Disrupts Valuation, Coproduction, and the Relationship Between Market Actors](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222429241296459) — Studies sponsored content by brands and creators as an object of an epistemic market, using qualitative analysis of interviews and media materials; generative AI is mentioned only in passing in the conclusion as a transferable domain.
- U095 [Automated Versus Human Agents: A Meta-Analysis of Customer Responses to Robots, Chatbots, and Algorithms and Their Contingencies](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222429251344139) — A meta-analysis of 327 studies on three types of automated agents (robots, chatbots and algorithms); the subject is traditional automated agents, and the abstract does not mention LLMs or generative AI.
- U096 [From JM as Catalyst to JM as Community: Positive, Bold, and Pragmatic](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222429251336054) — An outgoing editorial from the JM editor-in-chief team, reviewing the journal's positioning and publication outcomes, with no substantive content on LLMs or generative AI.
- U097 [From Tools to Agents: Meta-Analytic Insights into Human Acceptance of AI](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222429251355266) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): JM meta-analysis of 287 effect sizes on human acceptance of AI, framing AI as a tool or an autonomous agent. It concerns AI in general with no substantive LLM use, so it falls under the always-exclude list.
- U100 [Human or AI? Identity Salience Enhances Historically Marginalized (but Not Privileged) Consumers’ Choice of Algorithmic Service Providers](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222429261444284) — Studies how identity salience affects marginalized consumers' choice of algorithmic service providers (AI tellers, chatbots, robots, self-service kiosks); the abstract covers only general algorithmic services, with no generative AI or LLM.
- U101 [Climate Communications in IPOs: Unpacking the Influence of Climate Disclosure Volume, Sender, and Message Characteristics](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222429261444729) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Studies the U-shaped relationship between the volume of climate disclosure in IPO prospectuses and firm outcomes, using a BERT classifier only for measurement. - U103 [Book Review: New-Product Diffusion Models](https://doi.org/10.1509/jmkr.38.4.515.18900a) — A 2001 book review discussing works on the focus-group method and new-product diffusion models; it predates the LLM era and is unrelated to generative AI.
- U104 [AI for Customer Journeys: A Transformer Approach](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222437251347268) — Builds a self-developed transformer multi-head self-attention prediction model for customer touchpoint sequences; it only borrows the LLM idea of sequence modeling, and the model itself is not an LLM or generative AI; traditional predictive modeling.
- U105 [Welfare Implications of Democratization in Content Creation: Generative AI and Beyond](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222437261423540) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): A game-theoretic model of how democratized content creation through GenAI and similar technologies affects consumer and creator welfare. - U106 [EXPRESS: When AI Assists at the Frontline: External and Internal Marketing Effects in the Context of Banking Mortgages](https://doi.org/10.1177/00222437261483524) — Studies the effect of AI-assisted frontline employees in bank mortgage lending, but the thinking AI in the paper is defined as a machine learning tool that derives recommendations from historical patterns; traditional machine learning, not generative AI or an LLM.
- U108 [An empirically grounded analytical approach to hog farm finishing stage management: Deep reinforcement learning as decision support and managerial learning tool](https://doi.org/10.1002/joom.1342) — Uses a Markov decision process and deep reinforcement learning to optimize hog-farm slaughter sales; operational decision-making with traditional deep RL, unrelated to LLMs or generative AI.
- U109 [Estimation Using Marginal Competitor Sales Information](https://doi.org/10.1002/joom.1359) — Proposes a constrained maximum likelihood estimation method that uses competitors' marginal sales information for the hotel industry; a traditional econometric method unrelated to LLMs or generative AI.
- U110 [Generative AI and Empirical Research Methods in Operations Management](https://doi.org/10.1002/joom.1371) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): JOM essay on generative AI as a research assistant in empirical operations management research. - U111 [Developing Generative AI for Value Co‐Creation: An Intervention‐Based Randomized Field Experiment in a Healthcare Context](https://doi.org/10.1002/joom.70012) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Co-designs a generative AI intervention with a hospital and evaluates it in an RCT with 114 cardiovascular patients on post-discharge anxiety and 30-day readmission risk. - U115 [Mitigating Bias in Hate Speech Detection With a Small Number of Expert Annotations: A Prompt-Based Learning Approach](https://doi.org/10.25300/misq/2025/18416) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Proposes a prompt-based weakly supervised method that reduces annotation bias in hate-speech detection. - U119 [How Generative AI Transforms Questioning Behavior on Q&A Platforms: Evidence from a Natural Experiment with Pilot Usage of ChatGPT](https://doi.org/10.25300/misq/2026/19720) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses Stack Overflow's pilot and subsequent ban of ChatGPT as a natural experiment on users' questioning behavior. - U120 [FAIR: A Design Theory for Artificial Intelligence Fairness](https://doi.org/10.25300/misq/2026/17971) — Proposes the FAIR design theory for fairness tensions in AI automated decision systems, discussing AI fairness and governance in general, with neither generative AI nor LLMs as its core.

- U131 [Learning to Optimize Contextually Constrained Problems for Real-Time Decision Generation](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2020.03565) — Proposes training generative models with adversarial learning to produce optimal decisions; the generative model here is GAN-type traditional deep learning, not an LLM or generative content AI, so excluded.
- U132 [Deep Learning of Transition Probability Densities for Stochastic Asset Models with Applications in Option Pricing](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2022.01448) — Uses neural networks to approximate the transition probability density of stochastic asset models to speed up option pricing; a traditional deep learning numerical method with no LLM use, so excluded.

- U135 [Good Prophets Know When the End Is Near](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2023.04307) — Studies a model predictive control algorithm and loss bounds for online decision problems with exponential arrivals; operations research theory unrelated to LLMs, so excluded.

- U139 [ChatGPT for Textual Analysis? How to Use Generative LLMs in Accounting Research](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2023.03253) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Guide to using ChatGPT and GPT-4 for textual analysis in accounting research, validated on non-answers in earnings calls. - U140 [Who Is AI Replacing? The Impact of Generative AI on Online Freelancing Platforms](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2024.05420) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Estimates how demand for different jobs on a large freelancing platform changed after ChatGPT and image-generating AI launched. - U141 [The News in Earnings Announcement Disclosures: Capturing Word Context Using LLM Methods](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2024.05417) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Uses LLMs to capture word context in earnings announcements and explain short-window stock returns. - U142 [The Limits to Learning a Diffusion Model](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2022.02953) — Gives lower bounds on the sample complexity of estimating diffusion models such as Bass and SIR; statistical learning theory unrelated to LLMs, so excluded.

- U146 [Textual Factors: A Scalable, Interpretable, and Data-Driven Approach to Analyzing Unstructured Information](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2020.01180) — Builds text factors with word vectors, locality-sensitive-hashing clustering and topic models; a traditional embedding-plus-topic-model method, with the LLM treated only as a complementary comparison and not a method component, so excluded.

- U149 [The Effects of Generative AI on High-Skilled Work: Evidence from Three Field Experiments with Software Developers](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2025.00535) — Excluded on re-screening under the marketing rule (2026-11-16): Three field experiments with software developers on the productivity effects of AI coding assistants. - U150 [Firm-Level Litigation Risk: Measurement and Effects](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2024.05821) — Uses SEC 10-K text to construct a simple, transparent litigation risk measure; the AI-generated litigation risk measure is used only as a comparison benchmark, and the LLM is not a tool or method of this paper, so excluded.
- U151 [Does AI Cheapen Talk? - U152 [Learning Product Improvement from Consumer Evaluations](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2025.00055) — Uses decision trees and forests with a custom objective function to derive product-improvement suggestions from consumer reviews. - U153 [A Nonparametric Approach with Marginals for Modeling Consumer Choice](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2023.02287) — Derives consistency conditions for consumer choice under the marginal distribution model (MDM) with linear-programming verification; choice-model theory unrelated to LLMs or generative AI, excluded.
- U154 [Attention in Motion: Using Scroll Tracking to Measure the Dynamics of Preferential Choice](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.2024.06264) — Proposes scroll tracking, a process-tracing method for measuring preference choice dynamics; unrelated to LLMs or generative AI, so excluded.

- U165 [Fixing Inventory Inaccuracies at Scale](https://doi.org/10.1287/msom.2023.0146) — Treats inventory record inaccuracy as an anomaly detection problem in low-rank Poisson matrices and gives algorithms; unrelated to LLMs or generative AI, excluded.
